    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (72.2%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (72.2%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed

          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (73.7%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (73.7%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.1 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.83 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.42 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 67.63%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.1 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.84 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.42 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (71.9%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (71.9%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (73.8%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 70.75%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.4 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.65 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.35 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 70.53%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.4 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.81 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.35 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    Table Name : Warps Per Scheduler

    ------------------------------- ----------- ------------
    Issued Warp Per Scheduler                           0.30
    Theoretical Warps Per Scheduler        warp           16

    Active Warps Per Scheduler             warp        15.74
    Eligible Warps Per Scheduler           warp         0.36
    ------------------------------- ----------- ------------

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 69.84%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.3 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.74 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.36 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 28.87%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.7 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 13.15 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 2.70 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   ALU is the highest-utilized pipeline (74.9%) based on elapsed cycles in the workload, taking into account the 
          rates of its different instructions. It executes integer and logic operations. The pipeline is                
          well-utilized, but might become a bottleneck if more work is added. Based on the number of executed           
          instructions, the highest utilized pipeline (74.9%) is ALU. It executes integer and logic operations.         
          Comparing the two, the overall pipeline utilization appears to be caused by frequent, low-latency             
          instructions. See the Profiling Guide                                                                         
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-decoder) or hover over the          
          pipeline name to understand the workloads handled by each pipeline. The Instruction Statistics section shows  
          the mix of executed instructions for this workload. Check the Warp State Statistics section for which         
          reasons cause warps to stall.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 25.55%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 1.7 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 13.20 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 3.02 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 53.21%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.9 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.63 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.30 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 52.22%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 3.8 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.62 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.32 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.

    Table Name : Warps Per Scheduler
    ------------------------------- ----------- ------------
    Metric Name                     Metric Unit Metric Value
    ------------------------------- ----------- ------------
    Issued Warp Per Scheduler                           0.19
    Theoretical Warps Per Scheduler        warp           16
    GPU Maximum Warps Per Scheduler        warp           16
    Active Warps Per Scheduler             warp        15.68
    Eligible Warps Per Scheduler           warp         0.22
    ------------------------------- ----------- ------------

    OPT   Est. Local Speedup: 21.95%                                                                                    
          Every scheduler is capable of issuing one instruction per cycle, but for this workload each scheduler only    
          issues an instruction every 5.2 cycles. This might leave hardware resources underutilized and may lead to     
          less optimal performance. Out of the maximum of 16 warps per scheduler, this workload allocates an average    
          of 15.68 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.22 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible      
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, avoid possible load imbalances due to highly different execution durations per      
          warp. Reducing stalls indicated on the Warp State Statistics and Source Counters sections can help, too.
